People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Um, well... I hope you don't think I'm doing it on purpose or anything, because I know how important it is to take care of myself. But, um, I guess I haven't been eating as much as I should.

Um... oh, I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself, I really don't. But... yes. I guess I do feel that way a lot.

Um... well, I really hate to complain about being tired, because I know everyone gets tired, especially working construction, right? I mean, it's just part of the job, and I don't want to sound lazy or like I'm not pulling my weight with the crew. I really do try to push through it.

I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any specific plans about how you might end your life, or have you thought about taking any steps toward hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, please don't worry. I haven't made any real plans or taken any steps like that. I'm so sorry if I alarmed you, I really didn't mean to make it sound like an emergency. \n\nIt's just... like I was trying to explain a second ago, it's more of a heavy feeling that just sits with me. I just keep having those thoughts that it would be better if I didn't wake up. It's not that I'm actively trying to hurt myself, it just feels like... well, like dying would be the fair punishment for everything I've put my family and my crew through. \n\nBut I haven't planned out how to do anything. I'm trying really hard to be good and stay safe. I hope that puts your mind at ease a little bit? I really don't want to be a burden to you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest or taking less pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have been a little less active lately. I really don't want to sound lazy or anything, I promise I usually work really hard. But... yeah, I suppose I have. \n\nI used to do a lot of things. Like, I used to paint, and I'd cook these big meals, and I'd even go for a run before heading to my flooring jobs. I used to always show up for my theater rehearsals, too. But now... I hope this doesn't make me sound awful, but... those things just feel like chores from somebody else's life. I just... I don't think so? I mean, I hope I'm not coming across as depressed or, you know, ungrateful or anything. I really try to keep a smile on and be pleasant for everyone. \n\nI guess... I mean, I do have those heavy thoughts I mentioned, but I wouldn't say I'm just sitting around feeling sad all day every day. I really try my best to keep going and not let things get me down too much. I don't want to be a downer for the people around me, you know? \n\nSo, no, I don't think I feel depressed most of the day. Is that... is that okay? I hope I'm not misunderstanding what you're asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately, like eating much less or much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, well... It's just... most days I kind of forget to eat until it gets really late. I really am trying to eat when I can, though. I hope that's not too concerning? I'll try to do better if you think I need to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you're a burden to others more often lately?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, I really hate to admit this. I can't help but feel like everything going wrong is my fault. Like... my husband hasn't been doing well lately, and I keep thinking it's because of me. Like I just poison everything I touch right now. \n\nIt makes me feel like I just deserve whatever bad things happen to me, you know? Because I'm dragging everyone else down. Installing flooring is pretty physical, so I have to keep moving, you know? \n\nI haven't noticed myself slowing down or, like, pacing around or anything like that. I really try to stay steady and just do what needs to be done so I'm not a bother to anyone. If I have been acting different, nobody's mentioned it to me. I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and how often that happened over the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I should be stronger than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or low on energy lately, even if you’re not doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really hate to complain about being tired, because I know everyone gets tired, especially working construction, right? I don't know if I'd say it's been most of the day, every day for two whole weeks. That sounds so... so bad, doesn't it? \n\nI mean, I still have good moments. I really am trying to snap out of it, though. I hope it's okay that I feel this way right now? I know I should be stronger than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or low on energy lately, even if you’re not doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really hate to complain about being tired, because I know everyone gets tired, especially working construction, right? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I feel like we're kind of going in circles, and I really hope I'm not frustrating you by not giving a clear enough answer. I promise I'm trying to be as helpful as I can. 

I just... I don't think it's been *every* single day, or even nearly every day. Like I said, I really try to keep my chin up and focus on the work. I mean, if I was just sad all day, every day, I wouldn't be able to get any floors done, right? And I really pride myself on doing a good job for the clients. 

So, no, I wouldn't say it's been that constant. I try really hard not to let myself get stuck in a rut like that. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart when I'm still trying my best to hold it together.

Um... well, the losing interest part... I really hate to admit this, because I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or being lazy outside of work. I promise I'm usually a very active person.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I feel terrible, I really do, because I know you keep asking about feeling sad or down, and I feel like I'm just being difficult by not giving you a straight "yes." I promise I'm not trying to be difficult. 

It's just... I really don't think I've been feeling sad for most of the day, you know? Like I mentioned before, I'm still getting up and going to work, and I still feel okay when I'm there. I wouldn't want you to write down that I'm just miserable all the time, because that's not really how it is. 

I guess I'm just... I'm more focused on how I've stopped doing my hobbies, like we just talked about. But the actual feeling sad part... no, I really don't think it's been happening like that. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture of what's going on, without making it seem like I'm completely helpless. Am I making sense?

I hope I'm not sounding too crazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so? I mean, I hope I haven't been acting strange or anything. I really try to keep my energy up and stay focused, especially at work. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had a period of time in the past two weeks where you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, more days than not, even when there wasn’t a specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um... well, the losing interest part... Like, I used to paint, and I'd always cook these really big meals... I'd even go for a run before heading to a job site in the mornings, and I'm supposed to show up for theater rehearsals... \n\nBut... yeah, I guess I have kind of stopped doing all of that. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... I feel so bad that we keep coming back to this. I really hope you don't think I'm being stubborn or trying to hide anything from you. I know you're just trying to get the full picture, and I want to be a good patient for you, I really do.

But... no, even when things are fine at work or at home, I just don't have those long stretches of feeling sad or down like that. I know I've lost interest in my painting and the theater stuff, which is still so embarrassing to admit, but the actual sadness... it just isn't there most of the day. 

I'm so sorry if that's not the answer you're looking for. I just really want to be honest with you so I don't give you the wrong impression about how I'm handling things. Is it okay that I just don't feel sad like that, even with the other stuff going on? I hope I'm still giving you what you need to figure this out.

I hope I'm not sounding too crazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so? But, um, I guess I haven't been eating as much as I should. \n\nIt's not that I'm trying to lose weight or be difficult, I promise! It's just... most days I kind of forget to eat until it gets really late. Or, if I do try to make something, I'll take a few bites and my stomach just feels sort of sick, so I stop. \n\nI did notice my work pants are getting a little loose, which I feel bad about because I don't want to look unprofessional on the job. I really am trying to eat when I can, though. I hope that's not too concerning? I'll try to do better if you think I need to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you're a burden to others more often lately?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself, I really don't. But... yes. I guess I do feel that way a lot. \n\nI just... I can't help but feel like everything going wrong is my fault. Like... my husband hasn't been doing well lately, and I keep thinking it's because of me. And the crew I work with... we lost some jobs, and some of the clients' floors got messed up, and... I just feel like I'm the reason for all of it. Like I just poison everything I touch right now. \n\nIt makes me feel like I just deserve whatever bad things happen to me, you know? Because I'm dragging everyone else down. I'm really sorry, I know that sounds so dramatic and I shouldn't think like that. I really try to be helpful and do a good job, I promise. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so? I mean, I hope I haven't been acting strange or anything. I really try to keep my energy up and stay focused, especially at work. Installing flooring is pretty physical, so I have to keep moving, you know? \n\nI haven't noticed myself slowing down or, like, pacing around or anything like that. I really try to stay steady and just do what needs to be done so I'm not a bother to anyone. If I have been acting different, nobody's mentioned it to me. Is... is there something you're looking for with that? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and how often that happened over the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... I really hope you don't think I'm just complaining, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for. I really try to keep a positive attitude, I promise I do. But... um, I guess I have had some days where I just feel... sad. \n\nI wouldn't say hopeless, exactly. That sounds so extreme, and I don't want to sound like I'm giving up or anything. I still go to work and try to do a good job on the floors. But... yeah, maybe for the last couple of weeks, I've just felt a little more down than usual on most days. \n\nIt's just hard to shake sometimes, even when I'm trying my best to smile and be pleasant for the clients and the crew. I really am trying to snap out of it, though. I hope it's okay that I feel this way right now? I know I should be stronger than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or low on energy lately, even if you’re not doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really hate to complain about being tired, because I know everyone gets tired, especially working construction, right? I mean, it's just part of the job, and I don't want to sound lazy or like I'm not pulling my weight with the crew. I really do try to push through it. \n\nBut... I guess lately, it's been a little harder than usual. It's embarrassing to admit, but... sometimes, by the time I carry just one box of tile in from the truck, or even just sit down and answer a couple of messages from clients... my body just feels so heavy. Like wet cement, almost. And my head just gets so exhausted that I can't even figure out simple things, like which pattern to lay next. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I really am trying to keep my energy up, I just... I don't know why I'm getting so worn out so fast right now. I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses to get out of working hard. Do you think it's normal to feel that drained from such little things?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if most days, for the past two weeks or more, you've felt down or sad for most of the day, even when nothing particularly bad was happening?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really, really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm whining. I know I said I've been feeling sad, but... I don't know if I'd say it's been most of the day, every day for two whole weeks. That sounds so... so bad, doesn't it? \n\nI mean, I still have good moments. Like when I finish a really tricky tile pattern and the client is happy, I feel good about that. I always try to focus on the good things so I don't let myself get too down. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm just sitting around moping all day for no reason. \n\nI guess I just... I try to stay busy so I don't notice it as much. I'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer without making it sound worse than it is. Does that make sense? I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times over the past two weeks when, even when nothing bad was happening, you still felt sad or down for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... I feel like we're kind of going in circles, and I really hope I'm not frustrating you by not giving a clear enough answer. I promise I'm trying to be as helpful as I can. \n\nI just... I don't think it's been *every* single day, or even nearly every day. Like I said, I really try to keep my chin up and focus on the work. I mean, if I was just sad all day, every day, I wouldn't be able to get any floors done, right? And I really pride myself on doing a good job for the clients. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say it's been that constant. I try really hard not to let myself get stuck in a rut like that. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart when I'm still trying my best to hold it together.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had a period of time in the past two weeks where you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, more days than not, even when there wasn’t a specific reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um... well, the losing interest part... I really hate to admit this, because I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or being lazy outside of work. I promise I'm usually a very active person. \n\nI used to do a lot. Like, I used to paint, and I'd always cook these really big meals... I'd even go for a run before heading to a job site in the mornings, and I'm supposed to show up for theater rehearsals... \n\nBut... yeah, I guess I have kind of stopped doing all of that. It's so embarrassing to say out loud, but lately... I don't know, all those things just feel like chores from somebody else's life. I haven't been going to rehearsal, and the worst part is—and I feel so, so guilty admitting this—I don't even really care if I miss them right now. \n\nWhich is awful, right? I know I should care. People are counting on me. I hope I'm answering your question right, I just... I really want you to know I'm not usually like this. I want to be doing those things, I just can't seem to care about them right now. Is it normal to just... lose the drive for things you used to love like that?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if, over the past two weeks, there have been days when you felt down or sad for most of the day, even when nothing bad was happening?\n\nPatient: Um... I feel terrible, I really do, because I know you keep asking about feeling sad or down, and I feel like I'm just being difficult by not giving you a straight \"yes.\" I promise I'm not trying to be difficult. \n\nIt's just... I really don't think I've been feeling sad for most of the day, you know? Like I mentioned before, I'm still getting up and going to work, and I still feel okay when I'm there. I wouldn't want you to write down that I'm just miserable all the time, because that's not really how it is. \n\nI guess I'm just... I'm more focused on how I've stopped doing my hobbies, like we just talked about. But the actual feeling sad part... no, I really don't think it's been happening like that. I hope that's okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture of what's going on, without making it seem like I'm completely helpless. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you have had several days in a row where you felt down or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can do your job. Am I answering this okay? I'm really trying my best to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if, over the past two weeks, there were several days when you felt down or sad for most of the day, even when things at work or home were going okay?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no. I know you asked me about feeling sad earlier, and I... I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm being difficult. I promise I'm really thinking hard about it. 

But even during this past year and two months, when I stopped caring about my painting and the theater... I haven't really felt *sad*, exactly. Just... empty, I guess? Like I'm just going through the motions. I still get up and do my flooring jobs and take care of my husband, I just... I don't feel the joy in it anymore. But I wouldn't call it sadness.

Is it... is it okay that I just feel the loss of interest without the sadness? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

But, um, I guess I haven't been eating as much as I should. \n\nIt's not that I'm trying to lose weight or be difficult, I promise! It's just... most days I kind of forget to eat until it gets really late. I really am trying to eat when I can, though. I hope that's not too concerning? I'll try to do better if you think I need to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you're a burden to others more often lately?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, I really hate to admit this. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around throwing a pity party for myself, I really don't. But... yes. I guess I do feel that way a lot. \n\nI just... I can't help but feel like everything going wrong is my fault. Like... my husband hasn't been doing well lately, and I keep thinking it's because of me. And the crew I work with... we lost some jobs, and some of the clients' floors got messed up, and... I just feel like I'm the reason for all of it. Like I just poison everything I touch right now. \n\nIt makes me feel like I just deserve whatever bad things happen to me, you know? Because I'm dragging everyone else down. I'm really sorry, I know that sounds so dramatic and I shouldn't think like that. I really try to be helpful and do a good job, I promise. Does... does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too crazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so? I mean, I hope I haven't been acting strange or anything. I really try to keep my energy up and stay focused, especially at work. Installing flooring is pretty physical, so I have to keep moving, you know? \n\nI haven't noticed myself slowing down or, like, pacing around or anything like that. I really try to stay steady and just do what needs to be done so I'm not a bother to anyone. If I have been acting different, nobody's mentioned it to me. Is... is there something you're looking for with that? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you felt down or hopeless most of the day, and how often that happened over the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... I really hope you don't think I'm just complaining, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for. I really try to keep a positive attitude, I promise I do. But... um, I guess I have had some days where I just feel... sad. \n\nI wouldn't say hopeless, exactly. That sounds so extreme, and I don't want to sound like I'm giving up or anything. I know I should be stronger than this.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or low on energy lately, even if you’re not doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really hate to complain about being tired, because I know everyone gets tired, especially working construction, right?
